`CrossesSessionBoundary` itself is untouched, and this raises §16.6's existing **P9** trigger rate rather than absorbing P9's fix. **D-3 — the `ConditionsExtractor` leak — FIXED:** that extractor re-reads raw CSV lines by absolute index and does not inherit `filtered`'s exclusion, so weekend rows were re-entering the prompt; it now re-derives the same weekday test from its own `Timestamp` column, same `TryParseExact` parse, same MinValue-first guard order. **D-4** telemetry extends the existing population console line rather than adding a `LoadStats` object. **D-5** UI mirror only, in `UI/TweakSettingsForm.vb` (`CountPopulationRows` + the `popKey` term) — **display-string parity: NO OBLIGATION**, stated explicitly; the tweaker has no snapshot line, card binding, CSV column or bridge field. **Two named traps, both guarded explicitly and MUTATION-PROVEN:** `DateTime.MinValue.DayOfWeek` is Monday, so the MinValue guard runs FIRST in `MatchesWeekday` — reverting the guard order made `A59e` FAIL (an unparsed row was admitted as Monday); the D-3 fix was likewise reverted and made `A59d` FAIL (weekend rows re-entered the regime mix, 50/50 instead of 100/0); the weekday filter itself was reverted and made `A59b`/`A59c` FAIL. **Fixtures A59a–e** in `verify/ordercheck`: weekday rows survive, Sat/Sun excluded (a); the `"|WD"` key change re-seeds exactly once off the LIVE pre-change key (b); a filtered-adjacent Friday→Monday window pins the D-2 burn via the exact absolute row span, not just the outcome string (c); `ConditionsExtractor` excludes weekend rows from the regime mix (d); the MinValue trap (e). Existing `A15e` updated in lockstep — its literal expected key moved `"NY|1"` → `"NY|1|WD"` (the row data is Thu/Fri/Mon, so `filtered.Count` is unaffected, only the key string). **Post-change observable, not yet read:** the count of `SKIPPED_SESSION_BOUNDARY` entries in `state.json`'s `round_history` after the first weeks of running — the D-2 measurement and the P9 trigger; record it, do not act on it here. Acceptance: solution + AutoTweaker + WhatIfRunner + CeilingAudit + BacktestRunner + OrderCheck build **0/0** Release (each run separately); harness **ALL PASS**, A1–A58c unregressed + A59a–e. Local commit — trader tests + pushes. ⭐⭐ **SURFACE 2 OF 3 EXTENDS THIS ROW (2026-09-07): `LivePerformanceTracker`.** Per §15's one-item-one-row rule — the [weekday-scope ruling](weekday-scope-ruling-2026-08-03.md) §2 names three surfaces and this is the same queue item, not a new one. **The perf strip's 3-day and week windows were mixing in Saturday and Sunday — sessions the trader never trades — into the success rate read daily.** ⭐ **A scoping read settled the row first: the "SEVEN `DayOfWeek` files" that made it look indeterminate decompose cleanly, and `LivePerformanceTracker.vb:560` is a DECOY — Monday week-ANCHORING for the UTC+8 window, not a filter. Counting files would have closed this row wrongly.** **Filter placed in `AggregateRange`, beside the resolution filter and the E2a WEAK exclusion — the seam whose own comment states the discipline: *"Storage stays permissive; the exclusion is applied here at display time."* ⛔ The eval cache is UNTOUCHED, no rotation, fully reversible.** ⛔ **UTC day-of-week, NOT the UTC+8 display calendar** — the ruling's named precedent (`CsvFeatureBuilder.vb:199-200`, *"the existing precedent to copy"*) reads the raw UTC stamp and so does the tweaker; scoping this surface differently would make the strip's population disagree with theirs. ⚠ **Consequence stated: the trader's Monday-morning window opens Sunday 16:00 UTC and that portion is now out of scope. The window defines the RANGE, the filter defines the SCOPE.** ⛔ **`MinValue` guard FIRST — `DateTime.MinValue.DayOfWeek` is MONDAY, so an unparsed stamp would sail through as a weekday; both existing implementations carry it for that reason.** ⭐ **`WindowAggregate.WeekendExcluded` counts the drops and the perf-strip tooltip renders `Weekend excl.: n=N`, mirroring the E2a `WEAK excl.` line — a dropped count that is never shown is a silent hole, and without the tooltip line the counter would be computed and discarded.** ⚠ **No weekend RATE is offered: weekend rows are out of scope, not a comparison population.** ⚠ **Expect FEWER rendered cells, not more — excluding weekends shrinks n against `performance_display.min_sample_for_render` (10). Honest, and predicted by the ruling rather than discovered.** Harness **335 → 336** (`A67a`). ⭐⭐ **`A67a` was REQUIRED because the existing fixtures prove nothing here: `A14f`, `A33b` and the E2a fixture all drive `AggregateRange`, but their data sits on 2026-07-20 (a MONDAY) and 2026-01-01 (a THURSDAY) — measured — so every one passes identically with the filter present or absent.** **Mutation RUN: disabling the filter fails `A67a` ALONE with `TotalRange=4 WeekendExcluded=0 success=3 failure=1` — 75 % against the correct 50 %, so the RATE moves, not just the count.** ⚠ **The fixture also caught its own author: its first draft used an `EvalOutcome` of `"FAILURE"`, which is not a real outcome (`SUCCESS`/`ADVERSE_HIT`/`AMBIGUOUS`/`WINDOW_EXPIRED`), and an unrecognised string falls through both arms silently.** ~~**Surface 3 — `AnalysisRunner` / `WhatIfRunner`, which have ZERO weekday awareness of any kind — remains open.**~~ ✅✅ **SURFACE 3 OF 3 SHIPPED 2026-09-07 — THE WEEKDAY-SCOPE RULING IS NOW FULLY DISCHARGED, all three surfaces closed.** **`analysis/AnalysisRunner` and `tools/WhatIfRunner` both filter now.** The ruling's own words for why it mattered: *"correct only because every published derivation pre-filtered by hand. Nothing enforces it; a `report --csv` on an unfiltered book silently includes weekends"* — and an overlay sweep could have picked a setting on Saturday tape. ⭐⭐ **TWO SEAMS WERE REJECTED ON EVIDENCE BEFORE THE RIGHT ONE WAS FOUND, and both rejections matter more than the fix. (1) NOT inside `FailureRateMatrix.Compute` — it has FIVE callers including `AutoTweaker` (already filtered upstream) and many fixtures; filtering inside a shared primitive would silently change its contract from *"compute over these rows"* to *"compute over the weekday subset"*. (2) NOT inside `ForwardWindowJoiner.Load` — it too has five callers, and `A46a` asserts an EXACT row count against it, so that change would have broken a fixture and re-scoped three others.** ⭐ **The pattern that survives is the one surface 1 set: LOAD, THEN FILTER, at each consumer.** ⭐ **ONE shared predicate — `ForwardWindowJoiner.IsWeekdayRow` — placed in `analysis/`, which every offline project links, so copies four and five were never written.** ⛔⛔ **THE MinValue GUARD IS THE WHOLE POINT AND IT IS COUNTER-INTUITIVE: `DateTime.MinValue.DayOfWeek` is MONDAY, so a naive day check ADMITS every unparsed timestamp as a valid weekday row.** Both pre-existing implementations carry that guard for exactly this reason; extracting the predicate is what stops the next copy omitting it. ⚠ **`AnalysisRunner` filters BEFORE `report.TotalRows` and the verdict counts, so every figure the report carries is weekday-scoped — not just the matrix — and `AnalysisReport.WeekendExcluded` records the drop (`TotalRows + WeekendExcluded` = rows loaded). `WhatIfProgram` prints its own excluded count beside the existing `pocExcluded` line.** Harness **336 → 337** (`A67b`). **Mutation RUN: deleting the `MinValue` guard fails `A67b` ALONE, and its own detail line explains the trap — `minValue=True (MinValue.DayOfWeek=Monday)`.** ⚠ **Fri and Mon are both in the fixture deliberately — the two days adjacent to the weekend, so an off-by-one shows up rather than passing on a midweek-only sample.** ⛔ **NOT fixture-covered, stated rather than papered over: the two call sites' own wiring. `AnalysisRunner.Run` is async and fetches OHLC, so it is not harness-reachable; `A67b` pins the predicate, and the wiring rests on reading.** ⚠ **`AutoTweaker` and `CeilingAudit` still carry their own inline copies — deliberately untouched, since `A59a`–`A59e` pin the tweaker's and re-routing a shipped, reviewed surface was out of scope. Converging them on `IsWeekdayRow` is a queued tidy, not a defect.** |

| **v68 · auto-run on start** (D1–D2, Part A) | 2026-08-21 | **New `auto_run.start_engaged` (bool, default `false`). On form load, immediately after `InitAutoRunControls` seeds the interval/trigger-mode controls, `MainForm` now calls the existing `StartAutoRun()` if the key is true** — `UI/MainForm_Layout.vb`, right after `InitAutoRunControls()` — the identical path a manual Start click takes, so there is no second source of truth for the interval. **Default `false` ⇒ byte-identical to v67 on every box that has not set the key.** Spec: [`collector-ops-tooling-proposal.md`](collector-ops-tooling-proposal.md) §1 (D1–D2 ticked as recommended). **Trigger:** a scripted deploy (Part B, `tools/ops/collector.ps1`, no engine code touched) produces a running-but-stopped app; without this every deploy still needs a human to RDP in and click Start. **Safety property (§1.3, verified before speccing, not assumed):** auto-starting the run loop **cannot arm autotrade** — `chkArmAutotrade` has no settings-backed initial state and `_autotradeArmed` is assigned only from the checkbox's own `Checked` property (`UI/MainForm_Layout.vb` / `UI/MainForm_SignalBridge.vb`); ARM is off by construction on every start and never persisted. ⚠ **This property is NOT fixture-tested and cannot be from `OrderCheck.vbproj`**, which does not `Compile Include` any `MainForm_*.vb` file — it is verified by reading the two files named above, recorded there. **An earlier draft of A58b carried a reflection check over `AutoRunSettings`** (asserting zero properties named `Arm`/`Autotrade`) that proved only the *settings* side has no such field, not that the property holds — it read as coverage it did not provide (review finding, [`collector-ops-tooling-spec-back.md`](collector-ops-tooling-spec-back.md) FIX 3) and was removed. **Routing (§1.4):** tracked `settings.json` ships `start_engaged:true` (collectors run hands-off); the dev box opts back out through `settings.local.json` — the same overlay mechanism `trade_store.enabled` already uses. `auto_run.` **as a whole block stays rejected** on the overlay whitelist (`interval_minutes`/`interval_seconds`/`trigger_mode` still move scoring cadence, unchanged reasoning); `start_engaged` is instead admitted **individually** into `SettingsLoader.AdmittedKeys`, the `network.*`-key-granular pattern rather than the `trade_store`-whole-block pattern. ⚠ **The proposal's own §1.4 escalation trigger fired during build** — the overlay whitelist did *not* already admit `auto_run.` as claimed — **and this per-key admit is the re-design it called for, not a forced fit.** **Tweaker fence unchanged:** `auto_run.` was already whole-block fenced in `SettingsDiffApplier.RejectedPathPrefixes` (HARD CONSTRAINT 14 comment label; the actual returned reason text is the shared `"HARD CONSTRAINT 11/12"` prefix-reject message) — `start_engaged` inherits the reject automatically with zero code change, confirmed by fixture A58c rather than assumed. **Display-string parity: NO OBLIGATION**, stated explicitly — auto-starting reuses the existing `StartAutoRun()`/`UpdateCountdownLabel()`/`btnStartStop` path a manual click already takes; no line is added, removed, renamed or re-formatted on any surface. **Fixtures A58a–c** in `verify/ordercheck`: (a) a pre-v68 `settings.json` with no `start_engaged` key deserialises to `False`; (b) `start_engaged:true` round-trips `True` through JSON deserialisation only — a JSON-contract check, not a test of the safety property above; (c) in the settings-overlay fixture group: base `true` + overlay `false` merges to `False` (`OverlayAppliedKeys` = `["auto_run.start_engaged"]` alone), and `SettingsDiffApplier.Validate` still rejects a tweaker diff naming the key. **Part B** (`tools/ops/collector.ps1` — the SSM-driven `status`/`fetch`/`deploy` ops script, plus the collector-replacement cutover procedure in the proposal's §5) is a standalone `.ps1` outside every `.vbproj`, tracked separately — `[no-engine-change]`. Acceptance: solution + AutoTweaker + WhatIfRunner + CeilingAudit + BacktestRunner + OrderCheck build **0/0** Release (each run separately — `prepush` mode needs a committed diff range this uncommitted work does not have); `verify-gate.ps1 -Mode local-fast` **GATE PASSED** (harness ALL PASS, display-parity clean, version-bump nudge satisfied). ⚠ **Reviewed** ([`collector-ops-tooling-spec-back.md`](collector-ops-tooling-spec-back.md)): Part A ratified sound and ships as this row describes. **Part B carried one blocking defect** — `Wait-DeployGate` used a bare `(if …)` PowerShell expression, which parses but throws at runtime on 5.1 (a class of defect a parse check structurally cannot catch) — **plus three lesser findings (an unverified backup-success marker, an overclaimed fixture, an unattended-MessageBox path on a hand-edited invalid interval), all fixed in this same commit before Part B is ever run against anything, including the test box.** Local commit — trader tests + pushes. ⛔ **DEFECT, found live 2026-08-22, fixed same day (no settings change, no version bump — code only).** As first shipped this collected **exactly ONE analysis row per process start, then nothing for the life of the process**. `InitAutoRunControls` sets `rbSingle.Checked = True` unconditionally, and **SINGLE poisons every branch of `StartAutoRun`'s mode selection, not only the interval one** — in `on_close` mode the bar-close watcher starts normally and `RunAutoAnalysis` then disposes it on its own first fire (`UI/MainForm_AutoRun.vb`, the `If rbSingle.Checked Then StopOnCloseWatcher()` arm), which also removes the feed-stall backstop because the backstop lives inside the tick that no longer runs. Only the final `Else` branch repeats, and it needs `rbSingle` unchecked. **A manual Start click never hit this — a human picks REPEAT by hand; auto-start had no such step.** Fix: `rbRepeat.Checked = True` immediately before the `StartAutoRun` call (`UI/MainForm_Layout.vb`). **Measured on the t2.micro collector:** one row at 11:12:03Z, then 175 minutes of silence with the WS tape still capturing normally (streaming capture is independent of auto-run — the book stops, the tape does not). ⚠⚠ **The deploy acceptance gate PASSED on that single row and reported the box healthy** — `docs/collector-ops-tooling-proposal.md` §2.6 defines acceptance as *one* new row within 5 minutes, which a single-shot start satisfies exactly. **The gate cannot distinguish "collecting" from "fired once" and must require two rows spanning more than one interval.** Neither could fixture `A58b`, which ships as a host-agnostic JSON round-trip and cannot reach WinForms state. |

### trim-2026-09-14-24 - §15 row - v67 thin-trade-window skip gate (2026-08-20), full cell

Source: `docs/DeribitIndicatorProject.md` lines 408-408 at git tag `doc-trim-2026-09-14-pre` - 4457 B - SHA-256 `8b9e2b9bd2e6f6bd6807c700e250fdf4c35dda86d3a0e9b11ac5f994d0653436`.

<!-- trim-2026-09-14-24 begin -->
| **v67 · thin-trade-window skip gate** (D1–D5) | 2026-08-20 | **New `scoring.min_trades_for_scoring_override` (default 0 = derived). Closes the one unguarded degraded-feed path on the trade window** — found by the 2026-08-20 degraded-feed audit ([`trader-tick-queue.md`](trader-tick-queue.md) §2). Spec: [`thin-trade-window-skip-gate-proposal.md`](thin-trade-window-skip-gate-proposal.md) (D1–D5 all ticked as recommended). **The defect:** every guard on the trade path tested `Count = 0`; nothing tested for a THIN list. `DeribitWsFeed.SeedAsync` re-seeds the trade ring from REST on every connect — one transient REST failure on that single call silently leaves the trade ring thin after connect (candles seed normally, `_connected=True` still gets set), so the health gate passes on a near-empty ring. At the measured ~1.4 trades/sec, TFI (window 30) takes ~21s to refill and MicroCVD (window 50) ~36s; a single trade gives `TFIValue` exactly ±1.0, clearing the 0.15 threshold at maximum magnitude, and a spurious TFI direction also opens the aggressor-velocity burst path. No skip, no log line, no CSV marker — invisible and unrecoverable retrospectively. **Mechanism:** new `ElseIf recentTrades.Count < ScoringEngine.MinTradesForScoring(cfg)` in the `UI/MainForm_Analysis.vb` skip-gate chain, reason string `"recent trades thin (n<m)"` carrying both numbers. The minimum is **derived, never a literal** — new `Public Shared Function ScoringEngine.MinTradesForScoring(cfg)` in `Core/ScoringEngine_Helpers.vb` returns `Max(indicators.TFI.window_size, indicators.MicroCVD.window_size)` = 50 at shipped defaults, unless `scoring.min_trades_for_scoring_override` is `> 0`, which then wins — the fixture-literal provenance rule applied at the source. **D1** SKIP the run (no CSV row), consistent with every other insufficient-shape gate. **D2** derived minimum + override, override default 0 = derived. **D3** NOT ruled a dataset boundary — expected skip rate near-zero, the v32 D5 candle-freshness skip gate is the closest precedent and took no boundary; **if the post-ship watch shows a non-negligible rate this must be revisited.** **D4** `ExitGuardEvaluator.vb` mirrors the same derived minimum and still returns Clear below it — never skip or throw, since it runs on an open live position. **D5** a trade-count CSV column is deferred to the absorption D-1 rotation as a rider, not built here. **Untouched:** `CalcTFI`/`CalcCVD`/`CalcMicroCVD`/`CalcLiquidations`, `IsDegraded()`, the WS connect ordering, `SeedAsync` (making `SeedAsync` retry/fail was considered and **rejected** — it would trade a scoring defect for a connection-availability defect). **Tweaker fence — HARD CONSTRAINT 28:** `scoring.min_trades_for_scoring_override` exact-match reject in `SettingsDiffApplier` (data-sufficiency plumbing, HC11 class, the retired `min_tradeable_move_pct` precedent) + `PromptBuilder` rule 28; sibling `scoring.*` tunables stay proposable. **Display-string parity: NO OBLIGATION**, stated explicitly per the hard rule — no snapshot line, card binding, CSV column or bridge field changes; the skip reason rides the existing SKIPPED surface. **Fixtures A57a–d** in `verify/ordercheck`: (a) derives to 50 at shipped defaults, reason string carries both numbers; (b) a count at exactly the derived minimum does not fire; (c) ⚠ **the hardcode-trap catcher** — `MicroCvdSettings.WindowSize` mutated to a non-default 80 moves the derived minimum to 80 (fires at 79, passes at 80); a build that hardcoded 50 fails this one, and it was mutation-proved: reverting the gate to a hardcoded literal makes A57c fail. (d) the override — 0 is byte-identical to derived, a positive value takes precedence, and HC28 rejects the override key while a sibling `scoring.*` key still passes. The `UI/MainForm_Analysis.vb` / `ExitGuardEvaluator.vb` live call sites sit outside the OrderCheck harness's WinForms/live-`MarketState` boundary (the A16–A31 class) — covered instead by the **post-ship watch**: read `_lastSkipReason` occurrences carrying `recent trades thin` over the first weekday-week; a non-negligible rate falsifies the `SeedAsync`-failure model and D3 must be revisited. Acceptance: solution + AutoTweaker + WhatIfRunner + CeilingAudit + BacktestRunner + OrderCheck build **0/0** Release; harness **ALL PASS**, A1–A56g unregressed + A57a–d; verify-gate `prepush` GATE PASSED. Local commit — trader tests + pushes. |

### trim-2026-09-14-25 - §15 row - gap repair heals downtime, plus DR-1, DR-2, DR-3 (2026-08-13), full cell

Source: `docs/DeribitIndicatorProject.md` lines 410-410 at git tag `doc-trim-2026-09-14-pre` - 4671 B - SHA-256 `20b7e58fda8eda9eeae30682f2f2cfba522500a3e3f1cb21e1b55576681d5598`.

<!-- trim-2026-09-14-25 begin -->
| **Gap repair can heal DOWNTIME — hole-derived repair windows, + follow-ups DR-1/DR-2/DR-3** (settings-untouched) | 2026-08-13 | **ONE ROW for one queue item, per §15's own rule — this shipped as four commits (`c6c6942` Part A, `91942d6` DR-1+DR-2, `5346bc0` DR-3, plus their packets) and the detail lives in the spec-backs, not here.** **NO settings keys, NO version bump — settings stays v66. ZERO scoring impact, NO rendered surface, NOT a dataset boundary** (the engine reads trades from `MarketState`'s in-memory ring, never the store). Spec: [`trade-store-downtime-repair-proposal.md`](trade-store-downtime-repair-proposal.md) (§7 D-table ticked in full 2026-08-12; §5a carries the D-3 that was ruled after the build). Packets: [`trade-store-downtime-repair-spec-back.md`](trade-store-downtime-repair-spec-back.md) · [`downtime-repair-followups-implementer-briefs.md`](downtime-repair-followups-implementer-briefs.md) · [`downtime-repair-followups-spec-back.md`](downtime-repair-followups-spec-back.md) · [`downtime-repair-followups-dr3-spec-back.md`](downtime-repair-followups-dr3-spec-back.md). **THE DEFECT, in one line:** `ResolveResumeCursorMs` seeded the fetch cursor from the file's **LAST WRITTEN ROW**, so once streaming reconnected that row was current again and every hole behind it read as "already covered" forever. **Measured: a 60.3-minute hole, 2026-08-11 08:59:56 → 10:00:12 UTC, survived seven hours and two scheduled passes** and is now past retention. ⚠ **THE OBVIOUS FIX IS THE WRONG ONE AND MUST NOT BE RE-PROPOSED — widening `gap_repair_lookback_hours` changes nothing. The cursor skips the HOLE, not the WINDOW.** **THE FIX:** new pure function `TradeStoreWriter.ResolveRepairWindowsMs` returns the `trade_seq`-bracketed holes BEHIND the tail as well as the tail; `BackfillTradeMonthAsync` gains an **opt-in** `repairHoles` so the blast radius is one caller. **D-2 — detection on `trade_seq` ALONE, never a time gap**, because a time threshold is a tolerance and *"a guard that checks a FIXED TOLERANCE rather than COMPLETENESS turns one bad fetch into permanent silent loss"* is this project's own lesson. ⚠ **The correctness property is TWO-SIDED — no MISSED holes AND no PHANTOM holes — and one-sided reasoning passes its own tests:** the store is not sorted, `AbsentSeq` is −1, and an unfillable hole must be clamped per-hole. **THE THREE FOLLOW-UPS, all found by review rather than by the gate:** **DR-1** removed `MinHoleMs`, a 2,000 ms width floor that D-4 itself had ruled in — a TIME tolerance applied to the output of a COMPLETENESS check, hiding every loss narrower than 2 s (the shape the still-open `ResetBufferState` race produces); the one drop kept is a range that inverts after clamping, and it is now logged where it was silent. **DR-2** made `ScanForRepair`'s truncation cut TIME-contiguous — it had removed rows by FILE position while the list was still in file order, which on an out-of-order store scatters interior gaps and manufactures phantom holes that win the `MaxHolesPerPass` ranking; LATENT, needing >500,000 rows in one lookback. **DR-3** replaced `BackfillTradeMonthAsync`'s empty-window return `CountDataRows(path)` (the WHOLE FILE's row count) with `0` — every healthy pass had been logging the entire month file as "rows appended", making the instrument that verifies this build's own prediction unusable. **Fixtures `A56a`–`A56g` plus an extended `A48d`, and ⚠ THE TEETH ARE PROVEN BY MUTATION, NOT ASSERTED** — five mutations on Part A alone, and ⚠ **mutation 1 (the only one the spec required) leaves `A56c`/`A56d` green, so a build verified to the letter of the spec would have shipped with the no-phantom half untested.** **Out of scope, named so they are not lost:** **Part B** (fire a pass on WS reconnect) is ticked but **deferred by §2.4's stop-and-ask** — gate G-1 came back "venue-wide Deribit outage", demoting it to a latency improvement over Part A's own 6-hourly retry; **do not open a Part B session until Part A has healed one real outage.** **D-6** leaves the pre-fix era's 7,471 gap runs alone. ⚠ **A falsifiable prediction, written before the deploy:** after one outage the hole is filled within `gap_repair_interval_hours` (6 h), provided it is inside the 20 h lookback. **If a hole survives a full pass cycle, Part A did not find the whole problem.** ✅ **DEPLOYED 2026-08-13 13:10:32Z, AWS `e551f15e-b245-4392-8e71-89e749636f1c`** — a restart edge, not a version edge. ⛔ **THE PREDICTION IS ARMED BUT UNTESTED — no outage has occurred since.** Verify it from the store's `trade_seq` completeness, **not** from the repair log. |

<a id="trim-2026-09-14-26"></a>
### trim-2026-09-14-26 - §15 row - trade-store write guard keyed on identity (2026-08-11), full cell

Source: `docs/DeribitIndicatorProject.md` lines 411-411 at git tag `doc-trim-2026-09-14-pre` - 11080 B - SHA-256 `ef8617c9a148c5080e2b5aeb1cb7397bfaaf111b0d3623eb61e688a95d98be12`.

<!-- trim-2026-09-14-26 begin -->
| **Trade-store write guard keyed on IDENTITY, not on a millisecond (settings-untouched)** | 2026-08-11 | **The streaming capture path was discarding 49.2 % of the live tape at write time. NO settings keys, so NO version bump — settings stays v66. ZERO scoring impact, NO rendered surface, NOT a dataset boundary** (the engine reads trades from `MarketState`'s in-memory ring, never the store, and nothing has ever been calibrated off the tape — so nothing needs redoing). Spec + build record: [`trade-store-write-guard-identity-proposal.md`](trade-store-write-guard-identity-proposal.md) (§4 D-table RULED all seven as recommended, trader-directed 2026-08-11; §9 is the build record). Finding: [`trade-store-same-millisecond-drop-2026-08-11.md`](trade-store-same-millisecond-drop-2026-08-11.md). **The defect, in one line:** `Core/TradeStoreWriter.vb:149` read `If t.Timestamp <= _lastTs Then Return False` — **a millisecond timestamp used as an identity.** It is not one. Deribit reports a market order sweeping several price levels as several records sharing one millisecond, so the writer kept the first leg and silently discarded every sibling. ⚠ **This shipped with v64 and survived the 2026-08-08 identity build, which fixed the READ path's dedup and left the WRITE guard alone.** **⚠ THE MECHANISM IS OBSERVED, NOT INFERRED — the §1 gate ran on the AWS box, 14:37–14:47 UTC, NY session, `tools/WsTradeProbe/`, 313 rows re-derived independently from the raw capture.** G1: 313 trades over **159 distinct timestamps**, **max 24 trades on ONE millisecond**, 70 timestamps carrying more than one ⇒ the feed DOES deliver siblings. G2: `trade_seq` 296083662…296083974, span 313, delivered 313, **MISSING 0, gap runs 0, duplicates 0** ⇒ **the feed is COMPLETE, so the guard is the SOLE cause and this fix is SUFFICIENT — no second workstream is owed.** G3: 159 accepted, **154 dropped, 49.2 %**. **Three independent routes agree:** 20 h streamed-vs-REST-repair **50.1 %** · `trade_seq` gaps on 431 identified rows **47.2 %** · live guard simulation **49.2 %**. **69 of the 70 multi-trade timestamps arrived inside a SINGLE notification batch** — exactly where `_lastTs` advanced on `Buffer` rather than on flush — and `accepted` (159) equalled `distinct timestamps` (159) precisely: one trade per millisecond survived, the guard's signature with no interpretation required. ⚠ **The loss was BIASED, not uniform, and that is why it mattered more than the headline rate:** the 24-trade millisecond was one aggressive sell sweeping 63904.00 → 63875.00, `trade_seq` unbroken, **239,990 USD of notional — the guard kept the first 50,000 leg and discarded 189,990 USD, 79 % of the sweep.** The loss fell hardest on precisely the aggressive multi-leg prints CVD, TFI and aggressor velocity exist to measure. **Mechanism of the fix (D-1(a)):** `_lastTs` and `LastWrittenTimestamp` are **DELETED — not demoted to a pre-filter**, because a pre-filter reinstates the defect exactly (siblings fail it and never reach the new check). `Buffer` now tests **MEMBERSHIP of a bounded recent-trade window** using the relation `DedupTrades` already defines — one contract, two call sites, no copies: an identified row settles on `TradeId` alone, an identity-less row falls back to `LegacyRowKey`, and **an absent identity is never a key** (keying identity-less rows on `""` would collapse them all into one — the original defect reproduced at greater scale inside its own fix). An identified row registers **both** keys, matching `DedupTrades` Pass 1's `claimedLegacy` arm. ⚠ **One deliberate divergence from `DedupTrades`, recorded because it is real and not an oversight:** `DedupTrades` settles identified rows first, which is what makes it order-independent; a streaming guard sees arrival order only and cannot look ahead, so an identity-less row arriving *before* an identified row sharing its five fields yields **both** on disk where `DedupTrades` would keep one — **a duplicate, which the read path removes.** **⚠ THE BIAS IS DELIBERATE AND ASYMMETRIC (§3.1), and inverting it is the whole bug:** a duplicate on disk is harmless (A48d already writes duplicates deliberately and asserts a read collapses them), a dropped trade is unrecoverable past Deribit's ~24 h retention — **so when uncertain, ADMIT.** **D-2 ratified — `trade_seq` REJECTED as a high-water mark** although it is the tempting fix (monotonic, O(1), drops into the existing shape unchanged): a high-water mark **is a tolerance**, and *"a guard that checks EXISTENCE or a FIXED TOLERANCE rather than COMPLETENESS turns one bad fetch into permanent silent loss"* is this project's own written lesson. The identity spec records plainly that **whether Deribit ever resets `trade_seq` was never verified**, and a mark on an unverified monotonic is the same failure class being fixed. **D-3** window = **20,000 trades as a public CONSTANT** (`RecentWindowCapacity`), not a settings key — no failure-rate linkage, nobody will tune it, and a key means a version bump and a boundary question for nothing. **D-4(a)** the window seeds from the **file TAIL** via new `ReadTradeFileTail(path, maxRows)` — one streaming pass with a bounded ring, the same single pass `LastTradeTimestamp` already made, so seeding costs no extra file I/O and never holds a month in memory. ⚠ Every failure path there produces **duplicates, never drops**: an unseedable window admits everything. **D-5** the existing half-tape is **left alone** — past ~24 h retention nothing recovers it, and a wide historical backfill would write REST rows over a window the store already half-holds with no identity on the old rows, which is uninterpretable (the 2026-08-08 lesson). **D-6** code-only, no settings key, no version bump. **D-7** the §5b band measurement is taken but does NOT gate the ship. **Fixtures A55a–g, and ⚠ THE TEETH ARE PROVEN BY MUTATION, NOT ASSERTED:** the guard was reverted to the shipped `<=` (including its disk seed and `ResetBufferState` reset) and **all seven FAILED** — A55a `accepted=1` want 2 · A55b `onDisk=3` want 4 · A55c `accepted=2` want 4 · A55d `accepted=1` want 10 · A55e `readmitted=False` (the bias inverted) · A55f `siblingAccepted=False` · A55g `accepted=1` want 2. ⚠ **A55b, A55f and A55g would each have PASSED on the unfixed code as first drafted**, because their stated job is only to stop an older property regressing; each was given a same-millisecond sibling pair until it failed. **A regression guard that passes on the unfixed code proves nothing about the fix.** Coverage: the defect itself on REAL Deribit rows (a — `439922656`/`439922657`, reused from A53e, which aimed the same pair at the READ path and passed); reconnect re-seed replayed 3× incl. through `ResetBufferState` writes once and new trades still land (b); a mixed identified/identity-less batch neither duplicates nor drops (c); the empty-identity collapse at the WRITE path — ten identity-less rows on ONE ms differing only in amount all reach disk, while an exact duplicate still drops (d); ⚠ a duplicate aged OUT of the window is ADMITTED while one still inside is rejected (e — pins the bias; reads the production constant rather than restating 20000); restart over a populated store rejects re-delivered rows but accepts a NEW same-ms sibling of the tail (f); an unwritable store still never throws and still guards (g). ⚠ **A48b, A48d and A48e passed under BOTH the fix and the mutation** — those fixtures are structurally blind to this defect, which is the finding restated as a measurement. **⚠ THE FIXTURE LESSON, worth more than the code:** the defect ran ten days inside a path covered by eight fixtures, one of them named *"monotonic guard"*, and none noticed — because every A48 trade is built `A48Ms(i * 1000L)`, **one SECOND apart**, so no two distinct trades were ever put on the same millisecond. **A fixture that does not put two distinct trades on one millisecond does not test this.** A48b's comment and `Check` label are corrected in this commit for the same reason. **Display-string parity: NO OBLIGATION**, stated explicitly per the hard rule — the trade store has no rendered surface at all: no snapshot line, no card binding, no CSV column, no bridge field. The gate agrees (`no snapshot/card drift detected`). **Reversibility:** code-only and additive in behaviour — reverting the file restores the shipped guard exactly, and rows already written stay readable since the read path's dedup is unchanged and was already tolerant of duplicates. No settings key moves, so no config to roll back and no dataset boundary. **Two corrections to the spec's own text, in §9.4:** D-6's expected version-bump WARN did **not** appear, because `verify-gate.ps1` line 126 tests **committed** paths and the gate was run with the work uncommitted — it will fire on the pre-push hook instead, expected and non-blocking; and D-3's cost figures were understated (**≈5–10 h** of tape, not ≈4 h; **a few MB**, not ~1 MB) — both wrong in the harmless direction, so the ruling stands. **Out of scope, named so they are not lost:** **F2**, the `ResetBufferState` race at `DeribitWsFeed.vb:298` (a different bug in the same file — occasional, where this was deterministic); **`LastTradeTimestamp` reading the last LINE rather than the MAXIMUM** (the store already holds one out-of-order block, so the invariant is already violated once — now documented at the function, harmless, and no longer touching the write guard); a restart in the first moments of a new month may re-admit a few rows from the previous month's tail (duplicates, safe direction, commented not fixed). **⚠ A testable prediction, written down before the deploy:** since G2 shows the feed loses nothing, fixing the guard should take store completeness to **~100 %**, not to some partial improvement. **If a post-fix `trade_seq` check still shows gaps, something else is wrong and this build did not find it.** Acceptance: solution + AutoTweaker + WhatIfRunner + CeilingAudit + BacktestRunner + OrderCheck build **0/0** Release; harness **ALL PASS**, A1–A53h unregressed + A55a–g; verify-gate `prepush` **GATE PASSED**, 0 warnings. ⚠ **THE AWS REDEPLOY IS PART OF THIS CHANGE, NOT A FOLLOW-UP** — `settings.json` does not move, so **AWS keeps discarding ~half the tape until it gets the new binary, and that tape is unrecoverable past ~24 h.** Follow `aws-collector-deploy-checklist.md` §1.2's six-item allowlist; record the new InstanceId in its §5a. ~~**⚠ NOT YET DEPLOYED as of this commit.**~~ ✅ **DEPLOYED 2026-08-11 17:18:36Z, AWS `a5d701ad-eea1-4ba0-97a5-2ea05274c8c5` — status corrected 2026-08-14.** ✅ **AND THE PREDICTION HELD, on a sample large enough to settle it:** the first post-fix check rested on 66 rows and could not distinguish 100 % from ~97 %; the 2026-08-14 copy-back gives **134,204 rows, `trade_seq` span 134,204, ZERO missing — 100.000 %**, a sample **2,033× larger**. **The write guard is confirmed complete.** |
<!-- trim-2026-09-14-26 end -->

### trim-2026-09-14-27 - §15 row - v66 OBV trend_gate re-anchor (2026-08-11), full cell

Source: `docs/DeribitIndicatorProject.md` lines 412-412 at git tag `doc-trim-2026-09-14-pre` - 6544 B - SHA-256 `6bc8b80b6bc299e7fa1c65665fea2c11ad7a2fe3036426ac7156f7ebcdc3ad27`.

<!-- trim-2026-09-14-27 begin -->
| **v66 · OBV `trend_gate` re-anchor** (D2) | 2026-08-11 | **`indicators.OBV.trend_gate` 18.0 → 23.0, one global key. ⚠ LIVE SCORING CHANGE AND A DATASET BOUNDARY** — rows after this build carry a less-often-directional OBV. Settings v65 → v66. Trader-ticked 2026-08-11. Evidence: [`candle-store-derivations-2026-07-31.md`](candle-store-derivations-2026-07-31.md) §2. **Why — a re-anchor of an existing intent, not a new design:** v33 moved the gate 10.0 → 18.0 from a **one-session** recompute putting `\|obvChange\|` p50 at 18.5, explicitly targeting ~50 % directional. Six months of store data moves that p50 to **1m NY 24.23** (n=16,943) · **3m ASIA 22.24** (n=10,320) · **3m LONDON 23.95** (n=6,450). Restoring v33's *own* design point puts the gate at ≈23, on 6 months instead of one session. **Decision-of-record, a negative result worth keeping: OBV needs NO per-session and NO per-resolution split** — the three p50s span 22.2–24.2, a ±4 % spread across two resolutions and three sessions, and the **3m/1m ratio is ≈0.95**, so unlike the ROC keys the ×2.1 resolution proxy does not apply. A single global gate is correct; nobody should spend a pass splitting it. **Expected effect** (interpolated from the derivation's own grid): directional share falls from **62.2/58.1/60.3 %** (NY/ASIA/LONDON) at 18 to **≈52/49/51 %** at 23 — about 9–10 pp less often directional, which is the point. **Mechanism — no code change:** `obvChange = (obvLast − obvFirst) / meanVol` is **dimensionless by construction** since the v31 F5 normalisation, which is exactly why one global value is comparable across sessions and resolutions. Consumed **only** by `IndicatorEngine.CalcOBV`, called from `UI/MainForm_Analysis.vb:526` (live) and `tools/BacktestRunner/ReplayLoop.vb:544` (replay), both from cfg — one seam, no copies. `ScoringEngine.Calculate()` reads the already-computed `r.OBVTrend`/`r.OBVDivergence` and never the gate, which is why no fixture driving `Calculate()` from `New EngineSettings()` is affected. ⚠ **POCO DRIFT FOUND AND FIXED IN THIS COMMIT:** `Core/Settings/EngineSettings.vb` `ObvSettings.TrendGate` stood at **10.0 — the pre-v33 value** — against a shipped JSON of 18.0, and the two had disagreed **since 2026-06-13**. **Latent, not live:** the app always reads JSON and the harness passes `trendGate` as an explicit literal, so nothing consumed the stale default. Moved to 23.0 in lockstep per the v60/v65 precedent; leaving it would have widened the gap to 13.0 inside a commit whose own convention forbids it. ⚠⚠ **"IN LOCKSTEP" IS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE 2026-08-11** — quoted rather than rewritten, per the convention. **There are FOUR copies of this value, not two, and v66 moved only two.** Still reading **10.0**: the **method `Optional` default** at `Core/Indicators_Structure.vb:44` (whose own comment is pinned to *"settings v31: 10.0"*) and the **fixture literal** at `verify/ordercheck/Program.vb:661-662`. Found by [`seam-audit-2026-08-11.md`](seam-audit-2026-08-11.md) finding **S-2**, which established that every indicator threshold has **three code copies plus the JSON** — and that **11 of 42** method `Optional` defaults disagree with shipped JSON. ⚠ **The app is unaffected** (every production call site passes the cfg value by name — verified across `MainForm_Analysis`, `ReplayLoop`, `ExitGuardEvaluator`, `LiveMicrostructureEvaluator`, and all three offline tools load real settings). ⚠ **The harness is not** — fixtures omit the argument or pin a stale literal. `A6_ObvNormalisation` pins 10.0 and still passes because its own arithmetic (`obvChange` = 48) clears both gates: **stale, not broken** — but also exactly why no fixture would notice a `trend_gate` drift, which is how the original 8.0 divergence survived two months. **The queued A54a guard pins POCO↔JSON only and would catch neither remaining copy.** **Sequencing — D2 goes ALONE.** The 2026-08-01 D-cluster ruling bundled D1+D2 behind D3; **D1 is PARKED** (re-derived 2026-08-02 — the *unit* is wrong, not the value), so no bundle remains. D2's gate was the D3 ASIA watch **reading**, which it did 2026-08-11: **PASSED**, fire rate 10.97 % over 12 fully-covered weekday session-days, same-side 89.47 % ([`d3-asia-burst-watch-read-2026-08-10.md`](d3-asia-burst-watch-read-2026-08-10.md)). ⚠ **Why shipping D2 does not corrupt that watch — verified in the tree, not reasoned from docs:** `AggrVelBurstRatio` is produced by `Core/AggressorVelocityAccumulator.vb` from the trade stream and `TFISignal` by `CalcTFI` over `recentTrades`; **neither `Core/Indicators_OrderFlow.vb` nor `MarketState.vb` references OBV at all.** Both watch criteria are OBV-independent and stay re-readable. What D2 *does* confound permanently is the ASIA **outcome** question — which the derivation §4 already records as never measured. **Tweaker: no new fence, HC28 stays free.** `indicators.OBV.trend_gate` is deliberately **tunable** — it is the control key that must PASS in A15f/A15h/A28d, which prove the sibling rejects do not over-reach. ⚠ Consequence stated plainly: **the auto-tweaker can move this value if it ever fires.** It never has (no tweaker state file, `settings_snapshots/` empty) and stays data-gated on a real >40 %-failure NY×1 window. **Display-string parity: NO OBLIGATION**, stated explicitly per the hard rule — no line is added, removed, renamed or re-formatted; the OBV breakdown note row and the MARKET STRUCTURE section render the same shape from the same fields, only *which branch fires* changes. **Reversibility:** set 18.0 back in **both** JSON and POCO ⇒ byte-identical to v65. **Fixtures: none added** — A15f/A15h/A28d build their own JSON literal (`trend_gate` 10) as a fence control, independent of the shipped value; the OBV behaviour fixtures pass `trendGate:=10.0` explicitly. ⚠ **Named gap, not closed here:** there is **no JSON↔POCO drift guard for OBV** — that guard exists only for the ASIA arming key (A52a), and its absence is precisely what let an 8.0 drift live two months. **Queued as A54a.** Acceptance: `OrderCheck` build **0/0** Release; harness **ALL PASS, 265 checks**, A1–A53h unregressed. ⚠ **Deploy discipline — `settings.json` hot-reloads, so a version edge can land mid-`InstanceId` and become unfilterable** (`trader-tick-queue.md` §3, the `SettingsVersion` rider): **stop → swap → start on both boxes**, per `aws-collector-deploy-checklist.md` §5a. Local commit — trader tests + pushes. |

### trim-2026-09-14-28 - §15 row - trade identity trade_id and trade_seq in the trade store (2026-08-08), full cell

| **Trade identity in the trade store — `trade_id` + `trade_seq` (settings-untouched)** | 2026-08-08 | **The store row gains two appended columns, so a stored trade finally has an IDENTITY. NO settings keys, so NO version bump — settings stays v65. ZERO scoring impact, NO rendered surface, NOT a dataset boundary** (no indicator, card, snapshot line, CSV column or bridge field reads the trade store). Spec: [`trade-store-trade-identity-proposal.md`](trade-store-trade-identity-proposal.md) (APPROVED 2026-08-08, D1–D7 all ticked as recommended); spec-back: [`trade-store-trade-identity-spec-back.md`](trade-store-trade-identity-spec-back.md). **Origin — the withdrawn 78.8 % completeness claim.** Merging the AWS and local tape books nearly DOUBLED volume over the tested window (78.6 M → 152.2 M) while ZERO timestamps were absent from either side. Root cause: the store row was five fields, `TradeStoreWriter.FormatRow` was what both the read-time dedup *and* S0's venue diff matched on, and Deribit's `trade_id` was never read. Three live consequences: two books **cannot be correctly merged**; genuinely distinct trades were **silently dropped at write time** (22,376 of AWS's 228,163 August rows were exact five-field duplicates and nothing could tell real from replay); and any venue diff was **uninterpretable**. **§1 verification gate, run first and alone (2026-08-08):** confirmed live that `trade_id` (JSON **string**, e.g. `"439922657"`) and `trade_seq` (JSON **number**, ~296 M, hence `Long` not `Integer`) are present on ALL THREE shapes — `get_last_trades_by_instrument`, `get_last_trades_by_instrument_and_time` (the endpoint the store's backfill actually calls, which the spec's §1 table misnamed) and the `trades.BTC-PERPETUAL.100ms` WS channel — and that a trade seen on BOTH feeds carries the same values. The escalation fork did not fire. **Mechanism (D5, the migration that isn't one):** `TryParseRow` already guarded on `parts.Length < 5` — a `<`, not an `=` — so **appending columns is backward-compatible in both directions**. No file rotation, no rewrite of existing months, and one file legitimately holds both shapes (§5, the normal case after the AWS redeploy). Header becomes `Timestamp,Price,Amount,Direction,Liquidation,TradeId,TradeSeq`; `FormatRow` is now the ON-DISK ROW ONLY and no longer doubles as a dedup key; the five-field prefix is extracted as `LegacyRowKey` and stays byte-stable. **D3 dedup — the whole point, and the trap:** new `TradeStoreWriter.DedupTrades` is the ONE contract all three consumers route through (`HistoricalStore.LoadTradeRange`, `CoverageReport.AccumulateHourStats`, and the A48d fixture). Two rows are the same trade iff both carry an identity and the identities are equal; if EITHER lacks one, fall back to whole-row equality on the five legacy fields; **never key on an absent or empty identity** — keying a legacy row on `""` collapses every legacy row into one, which is the original defect reproduced at greater scale inside its own fix. ⚠ **The spec's relation is not transitive** (a legacy row can equal two identified rows that differ from each other), so the implementation resolves **identity-first** and says so in the code: identified rows settle among themselves, then an identity-less row is dropped if its legacy key was already claimed. That makes the result **independent of input order** and errs toward not double-counting. **D4 venue diff:** `ComputeVenueDiff` returns a `VenueDiffResult` reporting **identity-matched and fallback-matched separately, never blended** — a blended number would hide exactly the ambiguity this build removes — plus the store's own identified/pre-identity split, with an explicit *"diff is NOT exact while this is non-zero"* warning. **D1 `trade_seq` — the field that outranks `trade_id`:** a per-instrument monotonic sequence makes completeness a **local computation**. New `CoverageReport.ComputeSequenceGaps`/`AccumulateSequenceGaps` report missing counts, gap RUNS (scattered loss — the shape S3's longest-gap metric provably cannot see) and longest run, with **no network and no exposure to Deribit's ~24 h retention**, so a month-old file stays checkable. ⚠ Rows carrying no sequence are counted and surfaced as **NOT CHECKABLE**, never as clean — a legacy store finds zero gaps because there is nothing to check, which is the worst possible failure for a completeness instrument. A backwards step is reported as a discontinuity and deliberately **not** counted as loss (whether Deribit ever resets `trade_seq` was NOT verified). **D6:** this SUPPLEMENTS S0, it does not replace it — a sequence proves continuity, only the venue diff proves the rows agree with the venue on content; re-specifying the suspended daily S0 job is explicitly out of scope. **One seam, three feeds:** `TradeRecord.ReadTradeId`/`ReadTradeSeq` are shared by all three parse sites, making §0 trap 3 (the feeds disagreeing on field shape) structurally impossible rather than merely tested for. They live on `TradeRecord` in `DeribitClient.vb`, **not** on `TradeStoreWriter`, because AutoTweaker / WhatIfRunner / CeilingAudit all link the former and none link the latter — a build failure caught only by Release-building every project, not by the app or the harness. **Tweaker: no settings keys, so no new fence — HC28 stays free.** **Display-string parity: NO OBLIGATION** — the trade store has no rendered surface at all; the changed output is the `BacktestRunner coverage` console/markdown summary, which is the offline-report precedent and not a parity surface. Stated explicitly per the hard rule. **Reversibility:** the two columns are additive and the `< 5` guard reads old and new files alike, so reverting the code leaves every written file readable; identity already written is simply ignored. **Fixtures A53a–h:** identity round-trips through the real store (a); a five-column legacy file still parses and reports identity **absent**, not empty, as does a seven-column row with empty identity columns (b); ⚠ the empty-identity collapse — ten identity-less rows differing only in amount survive as ten, not one (c); equal `trade_id` with differing content dedups to one (d); ⚠ the silent no-op — two REAL trades (Deribit `439922656`/`439922657`, captured at the §1 gate: same millisecond, price, amount and direction) survive as **two** (e); a mixed-shape file dedups under both branches, order-independently (f); the venue diff's two match populations reported apart (g); `trade_seq` gap detection — 100/101/103 reports 102 missing, contiguous reads clean, a sequenceless store reports NOT CHECKABLE, and scattered loss counts as three runs (h). **⚠ Two existing fixtures were UPDATED, not merely re-run — A48a (which pinned the five-column header and shape) and A49j (whose `ComputeVenueDiff` signature moved).** Both are called out in the spec-back, since an implementer editing the fixture that guards their own change is precisely the hazard §0 warns about. **Fixture teeth proven by mutation, not asserted:** injecting trap 2 (reader ignores identity) failed A53d/A53e/A53f; injecting trap 1 (key on a possibly-empty id) failed A53c/A53f **and the pre-existing A48d**. Acceptance: solution + AutoTweaker + WhatIfRunner + CeilingAudit + BacktestRunner + OrderCheck build **0/0** Release; harness **ALL PASS**, 265 checks (A1–A52a unregressed + A53a–h). Smoke-tested read-only against the real 74,989-row legacy store: the sequence line correctly reads `NOT CHECKABLE`, and the `153.1s` longest gap is unchanged — legacy-only data has no identity anywhere, so dedup falls through to the five-field key and behaves exactly as before. ⚠ **THE AWS REDEPLOY IS PART OF THIS CHANGE, NOT A FOLLOW-UP** — `settings.json` does not move, so AWS keeps writing five-field rows until it gets the new binary, and every hour in between is permanently unmergeable tape. ⚠ **D7 ticked RE-OPENS the local-sampler question after this ships; it is NOT authorisation to resume local capture now.** Local commit — trader tests. ✅ **DEPLOYED 2026-08-10 14:08:39Z, AWS `d8678d2b-94c4-4308-adc1-a88a51c2feea` — status corrected 2026-08-14.** Every tape row after that id carries `trade_id` + `trade_seq`; the five-field clock stopped there. |

| **v65 · ASIA aggressor-velocity arming** (D3) | 2026-08-02 | **`indicators.aggressor_velocity.sessions.ASIA.burst_ratio_threshold` = 5.5, previously absent — ASIA inherited the exploratory default 2.5 for classifier/TAPE-display/CSV while the SCORING modifier stayed inert there. ⚠ LIVE SCORING CHANGE AND A DATASET BOUNDARY:** rows after this build carry the burst-modified TFI vote on ASIA; NY (4.5) and LONDON (5.5) unchanged. Trader-ticked 2026-08-02. Evidence + D-table: `asia-burst-threshold-derivation-2026-08-01.md` §5. **Sequencing:** the 2026-08-01 D-cluster ruling puts D3 **alone and first**, ahead of the D1+D2 bundle — it *arms* rather than retunes, and bundling it with D2 (`OBV trend_gate` 18→23) would confound the one session whose watch justifies it, both pushing the ASIA upgrade path the same way. **Mechanism — no change to the scoring path:** threshold *presence* is what arms a session (`ExecutionResolution.HasExplicitAggrVelBurstThreshold`, the wire-in D1 auto-arm), so setting the key arms ASIA through the shipped v52 seam in `ScoringEngine_Calculate_Scoring.vb` Step 2. **The POCO default in `EngineSettings.vb` moves in lockstep (v60 LONDON precedent)** — the harness builds every cfg from `New EngineSettings()`, so a JSON-only edit would leave app and harness pinning different behaviour. **Data gate met comfortably:** 323 fires at the exploratory default against the ~150/session bar the 07-13 NY pass deferred res-3 on; n=1,489 ASIA rows over 14 session-days, pooled AWS-preferred book, weekday-only, frozen. **Why 5.5:** fire rate **9.7 %** against the ~10 % design point and inside the 8–12 % band (the live default 2.5 fires on **21.7 %** — one bar in five, the always-on failure mode the audit retired non-directional padding for); ASIA p90 **5.35** → nearest grid value, identical construction to NY (4.51→4.5) and LONDON (5.69→5.5); same-side TFI **91.0 %** over the ≥85 % bar. **Decision-of-record:** no ASIA-vs-LONDON split — the res-3 burst distribution is **one** distribution (every percentile within ±9 % on n≈1,489 each), `burstRatio` being scale-free by construction; ASIA takes LONDON's value, arrived at independently, which is itself the evidence the method transferred. Kept as **two identical per-session entries, not promoted to `default`** — the auto-arm keys on session-threshold presence, so `default` would silently arm every session including any future one. **⚠ Honest caveat, recorded not buried:** the W6-4 informational column is the **only** outcome-linked read on this knob and is **neutral at best** — `AggrVelBurstRatio` AUC **0.5179** (n=217), `AggrVelNet` 0.4654. It does not refute arming (NY/LONDON were armed distributionally too, LONDON's watch passed; a ±1 modifier on ~10 % of rows would not show strongly on ~220 test rows) but the distributional case must not be led with alone, and it sits inside the **three-instrument finding** (engine score AUC 0.5407, tier bands do not separate, structural placement 0.4683/0.5000/0.3221) — no further scoring spend without weighing it. **Contra arm effectively dead on res-3** at every candidate T including LONDON's shipped 5.5 (ASIA 0.21–0.43 contra/day, LONDON 0.16–0.42), so criterion (d) cannot discriminate: on 3-minute sessions this modifier is in practice **upgrade-only** — not a defect, but the §4.5 warning half must not be claimed as operative on res-3. **Coupling caveat binds:** 5.5 is derived *for* `fast_window_sec` 5 / `norm_window_sec` 120. **Engagement preview** (computed from the derivation §1, not separately measured): ~10.4 fires/day, ~9.5 same-side upgrades, ~0.29 contra, on ~106 AggrVel rows/day. **Post-ship watch:** fire rate ≈9.7 % and same-side ≥85 % over a **multi-day band, never one session-day** — ASIA's per-day rate spans **4.5–13.8 %** at row density ~106/day, so NY's ±2pp band does not transfer. **Tweaker: no new fence, HC28 stays free** — `indicators.aggressor_velocity.sessions.` is already prefix-rejected under **HC19**, re-verified. **Display-string parity: no new obligation** — the burst suffix appends to the existing TFI breakdown note row and the card binds the same `SignalBreakdownItem.Note`; arming ASIA only makes an already-rendered suffix *reachable* there. **Reversibility:** remove the ASIA key from JSON **and** POCO ⇒ inert again, byte-identical to v64, pinned by A28c's constructed un-armed arm; `scoring_enabled:false` remains the whole-feature hot rollback. **Fixtures: A28c RE-PINNED** — ASIA *was* the un-armed exemplar and all three shipped sessions are now armed, so no session remains to demonstrate S2a scoping; the un-armed arm is now **constructed** (ASIA's threshold cleared on a cfg copy), pinning the mechanism rather than an incidental session. A23f comment corrected (stale since v60). **New A52a** pins the JSON→arming contract in three arms — present ⇒ armed @5.5, absent ⇒ inert @2.5, and a **JSON↔POCO drift guard**. Acceptance: solution + AutoTweaker + WhatIfRunner + BacktestRunner + CeilingAudit + OrderCheck build **0/0** Release; harness **ALL PASS** incl. A28c/A23f/A52a. **Rider travelling with this tick** (queue §3 — it could not travel alone): the v64 reversibility wording corrected below. Local commit — trader tests + pushes. |

<a id="trim-2026-09-14-31"></a>
### trim-2026-09-14-31 - §15 row - v64 in-app trade-store capture (2026-07-31), full cell

Source: `docs/DeribitIndicatorProject.md` lines 416-416 at git tag `doc-trim-2026-09-14-pre` - 7115 B - SHA-256 `2388d96633fe3d3fb188cd1dbed793e62477436539233ee7119f295c7c264425`.

<!-- trim-2026-09-14-31 begin -->
| **v64 · in-app trade-store capture** (D1–D5) | 2026-07-31 | **Streaming raw-trade capture off the WS trades stream + an in-app gap-repair backfill. Settings v63 → v64 (one new top-level `trade_store` block); ZERO scoring impact, NO rendered surface, NOT a dataset boundary.** Spec: `in-app-trade-store-capture-proposal.md` (APPROVED 2026-07-31, D1–D5 ticked; **D1 ruled AGAINST the recommendation**). **Why it is urgent rather than nice-to-have:** Deribit's public trades endpoint serves **≈24 h** and refuses older windows (measured, `backtest-synthesizer-proposal.md` §7.3) while candles have no such cap — trades are the exception, and the one input the backtester cannot synthesise around. Trade-derived signals (CVD, MicroCVD, TFI, aggressor velocity, liquidations) can only be **re-derived under different settings** from the raw ticks; `analysis_log.csv` stores their *outputs*, which keeps the answer and discards the question (§1.3 — also records the scale reason: ~382 CSV rows/day vs ~60,594 trades/day, ~160 raw trades per row). With Tardis declined on cost (~$350–700/mo subscription, trader 2026-07-31), append-forward is the only path, so **every day without capture is permanently unobtainable flow**. The previously-planned external Scheduled Task had three failure modes that are each **silent and unrecoverable past 24 h**; the app is already up 24/7, already watched daily, and **already receives every trade** — so this is a *write*, not a fetch. **Mechanism — two deliberately redundant halves (§1), neither sufficient alone:** (1) **streaming (primary)** — `DeribitWsFeed.ApplyTrades` buffers each streamed trade into `trades_YYYY-MM.csv` in the **existing store format**, so `HistoricalStore.LoadTradeRange` reads it with no reader change; buffered not per-trade (60k trades/day would be 60k file opens); a **monotonic last-written guard** makes reconnect re-seed idempotent (`SeedAsync` re-seeds from REST on every (re)connect, so the same trades WILL arrive twice); **never throws** (the `SignalEmitter.TryWrite` / `liq_events.log` discipline). (2) **gap repair (secondary)** — the one thing streaming cannot do: it is complete while the app runs and recovers **nothing** from downtime, so a low-frequency in-app timer runs the existing trade backfill over a 20 h lookback (under the ~24 h venue cap). Not collapsible: streaming without repair loses every restart; repair without streaming reinstates the 24-hour deadline the build exists to remove. **Refactor (§2):** `HistoricalStore.vb` sits in `tools/BacktestRunner/` (outside the root glob) and owns a live `HttpClient`, so moving it wholesale would push networking into the feed path *and* the fixture project. Split instead — new host-agnostic **network-free `Core/TradeStoreWriter.vb`** owns file naming, monthly rollover, buffered append + monotonic guard, the row format, the row **parse**, `LastTradeTimestamp` and the resume-cursor decision; `HistoricalStore` keeps the network backfill and now delegates **both** its trade append and `LoadTradeRange`'s per-file read to the writer, so writer and reader cannot drift (the `ComputeSideLevels` "one seam, no copies" move). `BacktestFundingSample` hoisted out of `ReplayLoop.vb` into its own file so the root project links `HistoricalStore` for repair without dragging the replay pipeline in. New root-level host-agnostic `TradeStoreGapRepair.vb` drives the repair timer; `MainForm` starts/stops it beside the WS feed, **independent of transport** (`transport="rest"` ⇒ no stream, repair alone carries it). **D-table:** **D1 AWS-ONLY — ruled against the recommendation on a better ground.** The recommendation was dual-box redundancy; the trader's ground is that **the end goal is the app on AWS and not on the local box at all**, so dual capture builds for a topology the project is leaving — redundancy across a box you intend to retire is migration debt, not redundancy. Consequences the build honours (§7.1): repair is now the **only** recovery mechanism, so it **fires once on start** and then on the interval rather than waiting a full 6 h (a restart is precisely when a gap exists — a build requirement, not a nicety); and the store lives on AWS, so any trades-consuming study takes a store copy-back alongside the CSV copy-back. **D2** both flush triggers (30 s **or** 500 trades, whichever first — time alone loses a burst on a crash, count alone can sit unflushed through a quiet Asia hour). **D3** `store_dir` resolved against the **exe directory**, never the process cwd (the cwd is not guaranteed and a cwd-relative store would silently scatter files); the repo's own `backtest_data\` stays the local analysis store, populated by copy-back. **D4** 6 h cadence / 20 h lookback. **D5** the app owns repair. **Tweaker — HARD CONSTRAINT 27:** `trade_store.` PREFIX reject in `SettingsDiffApplier` + PromptBuilder rule 27 — data-capture plumbing has no failure-rate linkage, same class as `alerts.` (HC25) / `exit_guard.` / `live_strip.` / `signal_bridge.`. Prefix-safe: no other top-level `trade_store.` keys exist. **Display-string parity: NO OBLIGATION** — there is no rendered surface at all (no snapshot line, no card binding, no CSV column, no bridge field), stated explicitly per the hard rule. **Reversibility:** `enabled:false` ⇒ the fold early-outs and nothing is written, byte-identical to pre-build. *(Precision, corrected by the v65 rider: A48f proves the **gate** — that `enabled:false` performs zero writes with the shipped defaults pinned — while the fold's **inertness beyond the gate** is reasoned, not harness-proven. The original "harness-proven" wording overstated it.)* **Fixtures A48a–h:** round-trip vs the shipped reader (header + 5-col F2 rows + liq flag); monotonic guard (identical replay writes once, a fresh writer re-seeds from disk, newer trades still land); month rollover (straddling batch splits, header on create only); gap-repair overlap is a no-op (covered window ⇒ no fetch, gap resumes at last+1, retention clamp, read-time dedup); unwritable path never throws (blocked dir + locked file, fold keeps running); `enabled:false` ⇒ zero writes with the shipped defaults pinned; HC27 rejects all seven keys while a sibling `scoring.*` tunable passes and prompt rule 27 is present; store path exe-relative and stable across two working directories. Acceptance: solution + AutoTweaker + WhatIfRunner + CeilingAudit + BacktestRunner + OrderCheck build **0/0** Release; A1–A47b unregressed + A48a–h; verify-gate `prepush` GATE PASSED. **Rider for the trader:** `aws-collector-deploy-checklist.md` §3's daily ~30 s RDP glance now carries real data risk — post-build it is the only thing between an unnoticed app death and permanently lost tape, so add the store's newest-file mtime to that glance. **Out of scope (§9):** order-book capture, backfilling the existing pre-capture gap (nothing recovers it — the declined Tardis question), store compaction/retention (~2.4 MB/day, ~900 MB/year), and any change to `analysis_log.csv`. Local commit — trader tests + pushes. |

### trim-2026-09-14-34 - §16.6 P11 - ATR-band recalibration (resolved 2026-06-17)

Source: `docs/DeribitIndicatorProject.md` lines 541-543 at git tag `doc-trim-2026-09-14-pre` - 992 B - SHA-256 `68e4af2439f9c3278610eaab35323bb27200bf241b498563d3476eb8d6a62f2e`.

<!-- trim-2026-09-14-34 begin -->
**P11. ATR-band recalibration for the current price regime.** RESOLVED 2026-06-17 (settings v37): `static_ref` 115->38 + trader-profile §5 bands recalibrated to the live regime (1-min 20/55, 3-min ~42/115; resolution-dependent since v36). Original context below.
*Condition:* trader-profile §5 ATR bands (Low<80 / Normal 80–150 / High>150) were calibrated for BTC ~$80k–$100k (Q1 2026). BTC is now ~$62k with ATR running 13–68 — everything reads "Low" by the old bands. Surfaced by the 2026-06-13 ATR-confound finding. **Low impact:** the engine's live ATR reference (`DynamicNorms.ComputeATRRef`) is a recent-average, self-calibrating; only the cold-start fallback `indicators.ATR.static_ref = 115` (set as the old Normal-band midpoint, v14) and the profile's reference bands are stale. Mostly profile-doc + fallback-anchor housekeeping, not a scoring knob.
*Action:* update profile §5 bands + `static_ref` to the current regime; ~10-min settings/doc edit. Backlogged 2026-06-14.
